## 6. The Tending

- Key: the-tending
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/the_tending_flow.dart
- Tagline: Find who needs you. Do the labor.
- Overview: Three times per decan, see who is in your care, complete one specific tending act, and repair what was missed. The Tending is a low-burden thirty-day Ma'at flow with nine sittings: Find and See, Gather and Attend, and Stand and Restore. It is not a parenting app, a guilt loop for burned-out caregivers, or warmth-without-labor sentiment; it is a practice of care made specific.

- Enrollment copy: Not perfect caregiving - look at who is in your care and complete one specific act per sitting. The looking is the first labor.
### 1. The First Seeing

- Event number: 1
- Flow day: 1
- Section: Find and See
- Title: The First Seeing
- Timing: Day 1 · dawn + 30 min
- Words: Before writing anything: I look for who is in my care. I do not let need become invisible.
- Steps:
  1. Name who is in your care in this season: person, animal, household, plant, place, promise, or vulnerable responsibility.
  2. Write one perceived need beside each name. Do not solve the whole list.
  3. Choose one name that should not remain unseen this decan.
- Optional steps:
  1. If the list feels too large, circle only the name with the nearest real-world need.
- Source note: In the Osirian pattern, Aset and Nebet-Het search first — not because they don't know where Ausar is, but because searching is what begins restoration. Seeing the care field clearly is the same act: what cannot be seen cannot be gathered.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Aset searched before she gathered. She gathered before she restored. The search is the first labor — not the lesser one.

Words
"Before writing anything: I look for who is in my care. I do not let need become invisible."

Steps
1. Name who is in your care in this season: person, animal, household, plant, place, promise, or vulnerable responsibility.
2. Write one perceived need beside each name. Do not solve the whole list.
3. Choose one name that should not remain unseen this decan.

Optional
- If the list feels too large, circle only the name with the nearest real-world need.
```

### 2. Listen Without Dismissing

- Event number: 2
- Flow day: 5
- Section: Find and See
- Title: Listen Without Dismissing
- Timing: Day 5 · 11:00 local
- Words: I hear the need before I explain it away.
- Steps:
  1. Name one need expressed by someone in your care during the last week.
  2. Write the exact words, gesture, silence, or behavior that carried the need.
  3. Write the sentence without correcting, defending, or making it about yourself. The need does not require your interpretation to exist.
- Optional steps:
  1. If direct contact is not possible, observe one concrete condition that tells the truth.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Explaining away a need is not the same as addressing it. This sitting distinguishes between the two.

Words
"I hear the need before I explain it away."

Steps
1. Name one need expressed by someone in your care during the last week.
2. Write the exact words, gesture, silence, or behavior that carried the need.
3. Write the sentence without correcting, defending, or making it about yourself. The need does not require your interpretation to exist.

Optional
- If direct contact is not possible, observe one concrete condition that tells the truth.
```

### 3. Seal the Seeing

- Event number: 3
- Flow day: 9
- Section: Find and See
- Title: Seal the Seeing
- Timing: Day 9 · sunset + 30 min
- Words: What I have seen, I will not pretend not to know.
- Steps:
  1. Return to the care inventory.
  2. Mark each visible need as tended, partial, or unseen.
  3. Choose one unseen name to carry explicitly into the next decan.
- Optional steps:
  1. Send one brief message if seeing alone would become avoidance.
- Source note: Aset did not begin by restoring Ausar — she began by finding him. What has been found and named is what the second decan can work with.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The first decan closes with a status on each name: tended, partial, or unseen. Not resolution — status. The record is honest before the labor decan begins.

Words
"What I have seen, I will not pretend not to know."

Steps
1. Return to the care inventory.
2. Mark each visible need as tended, partial, or unseen.
3. Choose one unseen name to carry explicitly into the next decan.

Optional
- Send one brief message if seeing alone would become avoidance.
```

### 4. Begin the Labor

- Event number: 4
- Flow day: 11
- Section: Gather and Attend
- Title: Begin the Labor
- Timing: Day 11 · dawn + 30 min
- Words: I gather what care requires. Warmth is not labor until it acts.
- Steps:
  1. Choose one care obligation from the first decan.
  2. Name the specific labor required: call, feed, clean, carry, pay, schedule, sit with, protect, repair, or ask.
  3. Commit to one act that can be completed before Day 19.
- Optional steps:
  1. If you are burned out, choose the smallest honest act that does not deepen harm to you.
- Source note: In the Heru restoration pattern, scattered reality had to be physically gathered before standing was possible. Care that remains an intention is still scattered. This decan gathers it into specific acts.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Warmth without labor is a feeling, not a practice. This sitting turns care that has been named into the act it requires.

Words
"I gather what care requires. Warmth is not labor until it acts."

Steps
1. Choose one care obligation from the first decan.
2. Name the specific labor required: call, feed, clean, carry, pay, schedule, sit with, protect, repair, or ask.
3. Commit to one act that can be completed before Day 19.

Optional
- If you are burned out, choose the smallest honest act that does not deepen harm to you.
```

### 5. One Act, Done

- Event number: 5
- Flow day: 15
- Section: Gather and Attend
- Title: One Act, Done
- Timing: Day 15 · 11:00 local
- Words: One act of care completed is better than a feeling left unnamed.
- Steps:
  1. Do one small tending act now, or name the exact time it will happen today. A time named becomes a commitment. A commitment becomes the record.
  2. Record what changed after the act, even if it was very small.
  3. If the act could not be done, name the exact blocker without self-excuse.
- Optional steps:
  1. Ask for help if the tending requires more hands than yours.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The midpoint check is on whether the specific act was done — not on how you feel about the care.

Words
"One act of care completed is better than a feeling left unnamed."

Steps
1. Do one small tending act now, or name the exact time it will happen today. A time named becomes a commitment. A commitment becomes the record.
2. Record what changed after the act, even if it was very small.
3. If the act could not be done, name the exact blocker without self-excuse.

Optional
- Ask for help if the tending requires more hands than yours.
```

### 6. Seal the Tending

- Event number: 6
- Flow day: 19
- Section: Gather and Attend
- Title: Seal the Tending
- Timing: Day 19 · sunset + 30 min
- Words: I seal this labor by what was done, not by what I meant.
- Steps:
  1. Review the Day 11 commitment.
  2. Name what was tended, what was partial, and what was avoided.
  3. Carry only one unfinished care obligation into the restoration decan.
- Optional steps:
  1. If you owe repair, write the first sentence plainly: I missed ___. I will ___.
- Source note: Care is not sealed by intention. It is sealed by the specific act completed. What was partial is named as partial. What was avoided is named as avoided. The record stays honest so the restoration decan can start clean.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The seal closes with what was done — not what was intended, not what would have been done under better circumstances. That is what carries forward.

Words
"I seal this labor by what was done, not by what I meant."

Steps
1. Review the Day 11 commitment.
2. Name what was tended, what was partial, and what was avoided.
3. Carry only one unfinished care obligation into the restoration decan.

Optional
- If you owe repair, write the first sentence plainly: I missed ___. I will ___.
```

### 7. Begin the Restoration

- Event number: 7
- Flow day: 21
- Section: Stand and Restore
- Title: Begin the Restoration
- Timing: Day 21 · dawn + 30 min
- Words: I stand where care was missed. I repair what is mine to repair.
- Steps:
  1. Name one care obligation missed or only partly tended.
  2. Name the harm or strain created without exaggerating it.
  3. Choose one repair act completable before Day 29.
- Optional steps:
  1. If repair requires apology, make it brief and specific. Do not ask the person in your care to manage your guilt.
- Source note: Heru's standing was restoration, not domination — he stood up where the order had collapsed. The repair act goes toward the damage, not away from it.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Standing is not standing over. Heru stood where the care had been missed and acted from there — not from safety, not from distance.

Words
"I stand where care was missed. I repair what is mine to repair."

Steps
1. Name one care obligation missed or only partly tended.
2. Name the harm or strain created without exaggerating it.
3. Choose one repair act completable before Day 29.

Optional
- If repair requires apology, make it brief and specific. Do not ask the person in your care to manage your guilt.
```

### 8. The Check on Repair

- Event number: 8
- Flow day: 25
- Section: Stand and Restore
- Title: The Check on Repair
- Timing: Day 25 · 11:00 local
- Words: Repair is not declared complete while the need remains untouched.
- Steps:
  1. Check whether the repair act named on Day 21 has actually been attempted.
  2. Write what moved, what did not move, and what still belongs to you.
  3. If the repair is blocked by another person, write what remains yours and release what is not. You are responsible only for your portion of the restoration.
- Optional steps:
  1. If the repair is blocked by another person, write what remains yours and release what is not.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The check asks one thing: did the repair act move the situation? Not resolve it — move it.

Words
"Repair is not declared complete while the need remains untouched."

Steps
1. Check whether the repair act named on Day 21 has actually been attempted.
2. Write what moved, what did not move, and what still belongs to you.
3. If the repair is blocked by another person, write what remains yours and release what is not. You are responsible only for your portion of the restoration.

Optional
- If the repair is blocked by another person, write what remains yours and release what is not.
```

### 9. The Tending Is Complete

- Event number: 9
- Flow day: 29
- Section: Stand and Restore
- Title: The Tending Is Complete
- Timing: Day 29 · sunset + 30 min
- Words: Before writing any closing line: I have not turned away from those placed in my care. Speak it once. Then check whether it is true.
- Steps:
  1. For each name you held locally, write one private closing line: tended, partial, unseen, or repaired.
  2. Name one thing the labor of this cycle restored.
  3. Name one tending practice you will carry into the next cycle without making a vow you cannot keep.
- Optional steps:
  1. Name who tended you in this cycle. Care is not only what leaves your hands.
  2. If you share, share only the generic restoration line. Do not share names or care-list details.
- Source note: Aset's work was not finished in one period — it continued across the mythological record. What you close is this cycle's portion, not the whole account.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The cycle closes with one honest line per person and one practice that continues. Care practiced once is a gesture. Care that continues is a practice.

Words
"Before writing any closing line: I have not turned away from those placed in my care. Speak it once. Then check whether it is true."

Steps
1. For each name you held locally, write one private closing line: tended, partial, unseen, or repaired.
2. Name one thing the labor of this cycle restored.
3. Name one tending practice you will carry into the next cycle without making a vow you cannot keep.

Optional
- Name who tended you in this cycle. Care is not only what leaves your hands.
- If you share, share only the generic restoration line. Do not share names or care-list details.
```

## 7. The Kept Word

- Key: the-kept-word
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/the_kept_word_flow.dart
- Tagline: What you speak within your house determines its order.
- Overview: Three times per decan, name agreements and shared rhythms, bring one break to a direct conversation, and confirm renewed order in plain words. The Kept Word is a low-medium burden thirty-day Ma'at flow with nine sittings: Name the State, Bring to Process, and Confirm the Order. It practices speaking what is true inside the closest sphere while keeping the response of the other person outside your control.

- Enrollment copy: Decan 2 needs a conversation with another person. You name the break; you are not responsible for their response.
### 1. The Inventory of Agreements

- Event number: 1
- Flow day: 1
- Section: Name the State
- Title: The Inventory of Agreements
- Timing: Day 1 · dawn + 30 min
- Words: Speak Ma'at within your palace, so that those around you may respect you. An upright heart is becoming to a lord; it is the front of a house which creates respect for the back.
- Steps:
  1. Write the two or three people with whom you share the most significant household or working agreements.
  2. For each person, write the current agreements: who handles what, who said what, and what was last promised.
  3. Mark each agreement kept, drifted, or broken. Mark any drifted or broken agreement for Decan 2.
- Source note: Merikare's teaching placed truth inside the palace first because that is where order is most likely to be protected or corrupted. Proximity makes drift comfortable — the closest agreements are the ones where it goes unnoticed longest.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The agreements that govern your closest sphere are running whether or not you look at them. This sitting makes the current terms visible so they can be honestly assessed.

Words
"Speak Ma'at within your palace, so that those around you may respect you. An upright heart is becoming to a lord; it is the front of a house which creates respect for the back."

Steps
1. Write the two or three people with whom you share the most significant household or working agreements.
2. For each person, write the current agreements: who handles what, who said what, and what was last promised.
3. Mark each agreement kept, drifted, or broken. Mark any drifted or broken agreement for Decan 2.
```

### 2. Name the Shared Rhythm

- Event number: 2
- Flow day: 5
- Section: Name the State
- Title: Name the Shared Rhythm
- Timing: Day 5 · 11:00 local
- Words: Do not utter falsehood, for you are a balance. Do not go off course, for you are impartiality. If it wavers, then you will waver.
- Steps:
  1. Name one shared rhythm that used to hold the household or relationship together and has drifted or stopped. Not a dramatic break — the small regular pattern that used to create order between two people.
  2. Name who was affected by the rhythm stopping.
  3. Write one sentence about whether anyone has named the change directly.
- Source note: The Eloquent Peasant names the person as a balance. This sitting treats shared rhythm as part of the household measure.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Agreements have two kinds of failure: the promise broken, and the rhythm that quietly stopped. This sitting names the second kind.

Words
"Do not utter falsehood, for you are a balance. Do not go off course, for you are impartiality. If it wavers, then you will waver."

Steps
1. Name one shared rhythm that used to hold the household or relationship together and has drifted or stopped. Not a dramatic break — the small regular pattern that used to create order between two people.
2. Name who was affected by the rhythm stopping.
3. Write one sentence about whether anyone has named the change directly.
```

### 3. Seal the First Seeing

- Event number: 3
- Flow day: 9
- Section: Name the State
- Title: Seal the First Seeing
- Timing: Day 9 · sunset + 30 min
- Words: Guard yourself against the blemish of greediness. It creates dissension among fathers, mothers, and maternal brothers; it embitters beloved friends; it isolates a wife from her husband.
- Steps:
  1. Review each drifted or broken agreement.
  2. Ask whether the break came from changed circumstances or from someone taking more than their agreed share.
  3. Choose one agreement or shared rhythm to address first in Decan 2.
- Optional steps:
  1. Before the decan closes, tell the person involved that you want a clear conversation about one thing in the next ten days.
- Source note: Ptahhotep's warning about greed specifically names what it separates: fathers from children, wives from husbands, friends from each other. The inventory closes by asking whether greed was the mechanism, because it is the one the holder rarely notices in themselves.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Greed breaks agreements from the inside — not through dramatic violation but through taking a little more than was agreed, a little longer than was promised. This closing checks whether that is what happened.

Words
"Guard yourself against the blemish of greediness. It creates dissension among fathers, mothers, and maternal brothers; it embitters beloved friends; it isolates a wife from her husband."

Steps
1. Review each drifted or broken agreement.
2. Ask whether the break came from changed circumstances or from someone taking more than their agreed share.
3. Choose one agreement or shared rhythm to address first in Decan 2.

Optional
- Before the decan closes, tell the person involved that you want a clear conversation about one thing in the next ten days.
```

### 4. The Conversation: Name the Break

- Event number: 4
- Flow day: 11
- Section: Bring to Process
- Title: The Conversation: Name the Break
- Timing: Day 11 · dawn + 30 min
- Words: Deliver the message exactly as it was given. Observe the truth; do not surpass it.
- Steps:
  1. Write the specific fact: We agreed to X. What has been happening is Y. I want to understand the gap. Keep it that short. The message that is longer than this has usually started editing the truth toward a preferred outcome.
  2. Choose the least escalating medium that still counts as direct: spoken conversation, voice message, text, or written note.
  3. Send or schedule the message before marking this event prepared.
- Optional steps:
  1. If the conversation is not safe or possible, pause the flow locally and seek appropriate support.
- Source note: Ptahhotep instructs the messenger to carry the message exactly as given. This event applies that discipline to a broken agreement.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The conversation starts with the fact, not the feeling. This sitting writes the message before it is delivered.

Words
"Deliver the message exactly as it was given. Observe the truth; do not surpass it."

Steps
1. Write the specific fact: We agreed to X. What has been happening is Y. I want to understand the gap. Keep it that short. The message that is longer than this has usually started editing the truth toward a preferred outcome.
2. Choose the least escalating medium that still counts as direct: spoken conversation, voice message, text, or written note.
3. Send or schedule the message before marking this event prepared.

Optional
- If the conversation is not safe or possible, pause the flow locally and seek appropriate support.
```

### 5. The Conversation: Confirm It Was Had

- Event number: 5
- Flow day: 15
- Section: Bring to Process
- Title: The Conversation: Confirm It Was Had
- Timing: Day 15 · after conversation
- Words: The dispute between Truth and Falsehood was settled.
- Steps:
  1. If the conversation happened, write three private sentences: what I said, what they said, and what was agreed.
  2. If it has not happened, mark conversation pending and schedule it before Decan 2 closes.
  3. Name one thing that surprised you, if anything did.
- Optional steps:
  1. If no conversation can happen safely, keep the flow paused locally rather than forcing contact.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The conversation is the event — not the outcome. This sitting records whether the direct exchange happened, not whether it resolved everything.

Words
"The dispute between Truth and Falsehood was settled."

Steps
1. If the conversation happened, write three private sentences: what I said, what they said, and what was agreed.
2. If it has not happened, mark conversation pending and schedule it before Decan 2 closes.
3. Name one thing that surprised you, if anything did.

Optional
- If no conversation can happen safely, keep the flow paused locally rather than forcing contact.

Completion
If the conversation has not been marked complete, choose Conversation pending.
```

### 6. Seal the Naming

- Event number: 6
- Flow day: 19
- Section: Bring to Process
- Title: Seal the Naming
- Timing: Day 19 · sunset + 30 min
- Words: He was found still alive. The dispute was settled.
- Steps:
  1. Name the current status of the break: resolved, in process, or named but unresolved.
  2. If it remains unresolved, write the next concrete step as an agreement: who does what by when.
  3. Name one accurate thing the other person said that you had been holding differently.
- Source note: The Blinding of Truth by Falsehood ends with the buried dispute made visible — not with instant resolution, but with the hidden thing brought into process. What has been named but not yet resolved has already cleared the hardest stage: it is no longer hidden.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The naming decan closes with where the break actually stands. Resolved, in process, or named but unresolved — all three are honest positions.

Words
"He was found still alive. The dispute was settled."

Steps
1. Name the current status of the break: resolved, in process, or named but unresolved.
2. If it remains unresolved, write the next concrete step as an agreement: who does what by when.
3. Name one accurate thing the other person said that you had been holding differently.
```

### 7. Confirm the Renewed Agreement

- Event number: 7
- Flow day: 21
- Section: Confirm the Order
- Title: Confirm the Renewed Agreement
- Timing: Day 21 · dawn + 30 min
- Words: Observe Ma'at, that you may endure long upon the earth. An upright heart is becoming to a lord.
- Steps:
  1. Write the renewed agreement in one or two sentences.
  2. Name who needs to recognize it and how you will confirm recognition.
  3. Read the written agreement aloud so the word becomes operative. The spoken agreement is more present than the written one.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
An agreement that both people would recognize is a real agreement. The test is whether you could each read it back and say: yes, that is what we agreed.

Words
"Observe Ma'at, that you may endure long upon the earth. An upright heart is becoming to a lord."

Steps
1. Write the renewed agreement in one or two sentences.
2. Name who needs to recognize it and how you will confirm recognition.
3. Read the written agreement aloud so the word becomes operative. The spoken agreement is more present than the written one.
```

### 8. The Rhythm Check

- Event number: 8
- Flow day: 25
- Section: Confirm the Order
- Title: The Rhythm Check
- Timing: Day 25 · 11:00 local
- Words: Do not be selfish with respect to your relatives, for greater is the claim of the good-natured man than that of the assertive. He who forsakes his relatives is truly poor.
- Steps:
  1. Check whether anyone in the renewed agreement is carrying more than their share in practice.
  2. If the weight has shifted, name the small correction before Event 9.
  3. Name one thing the renewed agreement has already produced, if something has held.
- Optional steps:
  1. Make the correction short. Healthy agreements need small adjustments as they settle.
- Source note: Ptahhotep says the person who forsakes their relatives through selfishness becomes poor — not economically, but relationally. The rhythm check keeps the renewed agreement from drifting back into the old imbalance through small, unintentional taking.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
A renewed agreement shifts in practice before it shifts formally. This sitting catches the small drift before it becomes the next break.

Words
"Do not be selfish with respect to your relatives, for greater is the claim of the good-natured man than that of the assertive. He who forsakes his relatives is truly poor."

Steps
1. Check whether anyone in the renewed agreement is carrying more than their share in practice.
2. If the weight has shifted, name the small correction before Event 9.
3. Name one thing the renewed agreement has already produced, if something has held.

Optional
- Make the correction short. Healthy agreements need small adjustments as they settle.
```

### 9. The Kept Word Closes

- Event number: 9
- Flow day: 29
- Section: Confirm the Order
- Title: The Kept Word Closes
- Timing: Day 29 · sunset + 30 min
- Words: Speak Ma'at within your palace. The front of the house determines the back. Observe Ma'at, that you may endure long upon the earth.
- Steps:
  1. Return to your Day 1 inventory and speak only the current status that is true: kept, repaired, in process, or still broken.
  2. For the shared rhythm named in Decan 1, name whether it returned and what made it possible, or what remains in the way.
  3. Write one line that is now true that was not true at the start of this flow.
- Optional steps:
  1. If you share, share only the generic closing line. Do not share names, agreements, or conversation content.
- Source note: The quality of the palace determined the quality of everything that radiated from it — Merikare's principle. The Kept Word returns there: the closest sphere, re-examined, re-negotiated, re-spoken. What is now true at the front of the house will determine what follows.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The flow closes with one line that is now true that was not true at the start. That line is the measure of the cycle.

Words
"Speak Ma'at within your palace. The front of the house determines the back. Observe Ma'at, that you may endure long upon the earth."

Steps
1. Return to your Day 1 inventory and speak only the current status that is true: kept, repaired, in process, or still broken.
2. For the shared rhythm named in Decan 1, name whether it returned and what made it possible, or what remains in the way.
3. Write one line that is now true that was not true at the start of this flow.

Optional
- If you share, share only the generic closing line. Do not share names, agreements, or conversation content.
```

## 8. The Course

- Key: the-course
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/the_course_flow.dart
- Tagline: Know where you are in time. Act from there.
- Overview: Three times per decan, locate yourself in the solar day, the ten-day decan, and the active Kemetic season, then do one time-appropriate act. The Course is a very low-burden thirty-day Ma'at flow with nine sittings: Daily Course, Decan Course, and Seasonal Course. It is not an astronomy flow, a morning rite, passive day-card reading, or generic seasonal journaling; it makes the ḥꜣw calendar a practice document.

### 1. The Solar Course: Locate at Dawn

- Event number: 1
- Flow day: 1
- Section: Daily Course
- Title: The Solar Course: Locate at Dawn
- Timing: Day 1 · dawn
- Words: Face east, or face the window: Riser, Riser! Beetle, Beetle! Your life is related to mine; my life is related to yours. Sustenance is for my morning, Abundance is for my evening. Famine will not have control of this life.
- Steps:
  1. Open the ḥꜣw day card. Read the Kemetic date, decan name, and Ma'at principle before you close it.
  2. Name one thing appropriate for the morning part of this particular day.
  3. Do one opening act that matches the day instead of only the task list.
- Optional steps:
  1. Face east for a moment. This is the direction of return.
- Source note: Pyramid Texts Utterance 388 addressed the returning Sun as an entity that had just arrived and with whom the speaker was in reciprocal relationship. The day card locates you in the Kemetic count; facing east makes the location physical.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The Pyramid Texts addressed the returning sun directly because it had actually just arrived. This rite does the same — the day has returned. Locating yourself in it before the tasks begin is not metaphorical.

Day Card
Open the ḥꜣw day card before this sitting. Read the date, decan, season, and Ma'at principle before choosing the act.

Words
"Face east, or face the window: Riser, Riser! Beetle, Beetle! Your life is related to mine; my life is related to yours. Sustenance is for my morning, Abundance is for my evening. Famine will not have control of this life."

Steps
1. Open the ḥꜣw day card. Read the Kemetic date, decan name, and Ma'at principle before you close it.
2. Name one thing appropriate for the morning part of this particular day.
3. Do one opening act that matches the day instead of only the task list.

Optional
- Face east for a moment. This is the direction of return.
```

### 2. The Solar Course: Mark the Transition

- Event number: 2
- Flow day: 5
- Section: Daily Course
- Title: The Solar Course: Mark the Transition
- Timing: Day 5 · dusk
- Words: The Sun's daily movement is a journey from birth to death, and its rebirth at dawn is made possible by what passes through the night. I have completed the visible portion of this day.
- Steps:
  1. Open the day card again at dusk and note what actually happened between dawn and dusk.
  2. At dusk, stop one thing for thirty seconds and notice that the visible portion of the day has ended. Not as a meditation — as a fact.
  3. Name one thing completed and one thing that now passes into night.
- Source note: The solar course moves from dawn birth through dusk completion and the hidden night passage.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The solar barge crossed a threshold at dusk into the hidden passage. The visible day has actually ended. Naming one completed thing and one thing that passes through night is the scribe's record of the crossing.

Day Card
Open the ḥꜣw day card before this sitting. Read the date, decan, season, and Ma'at principle before choosing the act.

Words
"The Sun's daily movement is a journey from birth to death, and its rebirth at dawn is made possible by what passes through the night. I have completed the visible portion of this day."

Steps
1. Open the day card again at dusk and note what actually happened between dawn and dusk.
2. At dusk, stop one thing for thirty seconds and notice that the visible portion of the day has ended. Not as a meditation — as a fact.
3. Name one thing completed and one thing that now passes into night.
```

### 4. The Decan Course: Locate in Ten-Day Time

- Event number: 4
- Flow day: 11
- Section: Decan Course
- Title: The Decan Course: Locate in Ten-Day Time
- Timing: Day 11 · dawn
- Words: The sky's door has been opened to you. You shall set course to your proper work and make your yearly supply.
- Steps:
  1. Open the ḥꜣw day card before anything else.
  2. Read the decan name, ten-day theme, and Ma'at principle for the current decan.
  3. Name what this specific decan calls for in your life right now.
- Source note: The 36 decans were star groups that rose in the eastern sky at ten-day intervals, serving as the Kemetic clock. Each had a distinct character. This rite makes the current decan's quality something you actively work with, not just observe.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The decan is not just a period of time — it is a quality of time with a specific astronomical marker. Knowing which decan you are in tells you what the sky is currently measuring.

Day Card
Open the ḥꜣw day card before this sitting. Read the date, decan, season, and Ma'at principle before choosing the act.

Words
"The sky's door has been opened to you. You shall set course to your proper work and make your yearly supply."

Steps
1. Open the ḥꜣw day card before anything else.
2. Read the decan name, ten-day theme, and Ma'at principle for the current decan.
3. Name what this specific decan calls for in your life right now.
```

### 6. The Decan Course: The Arc Closes

- Event number: 6
- Flow day: 19
- Section: Decan Course
- Title: The Decan Course: The Arc Closes
- Timing: Day 19 · sunset + 30 min
- Words: I have hoed emmer for you, I have plowed barley for you: barley for your supply, emmer for your yearly supply.
- Steps:
  1. Open the ḥꜣw day card before the decan closes.
  2. Return to the decan's Ma'at principle and ask whether it was lived at all.
  3. Name one completion from this decan that can feed the next ten days.
- Optional steps:
  1. Read the next decan name and theme before the evening closes.
- Source note: The Pyramid Texts speak of agricultural labor as provision for the divine. The decan act does the same: whatever was done in this ten-day period is named as provision for the practice, for the record, for the cycle that continues.

### 9. The Course Holds

- Event number: 9
- Flow day: 29
- Section: Seasonal Course
- Title: The Course Holds
- Timing: Day 29 · dawn
- Words: Riser, Riser! Beetle, Beetle! Your life is related to mine; mine is related to yours. Sustenance is for the morning. Abundance is for the evening. I have ordered what I can in its proper place.
- Steps:
  1. Open the ḥꜣw day card. Let it be the first thing you read.
  2. Speak only the closing truth lines that are true: I know the decan, I know the season, I greeted dawn, I did a decan act, I did a seasonal act.
  3. Name one practice from these thirty days that you will continue past the flow.
  4. Speak the final line: The course is continuous. I am in it.
- Optional steps:
  1. If you share, share only one practice you will continue. Do not turn the private record into performance.
- Source note: The Course closes at dawn because the practice is not finished — it is established. The astronomer-priest did not retire; the decan clock kept running; the seasonal rites repeated with the year. The final line speaks from inside a continuing relationship with time, not from the completion of a temporary program.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose

Day Card
Open the ḥꜣw day card before this sitting. Read the date, decan, season, and Ma'at principle before choosing the act.

Words

Steps

2. Speak only the closing truth lines that are true: I know the decan, I know the season, I greeted dawn, I did a decan act, I did a seasonal act.
3. Name one practice from these thirty days that you will continue past the flow.
4. Speak the final line: The course is continuous. I am in it.

Optional
- If you share, share only one practice you will continue. Do not turn the private record into performance.
```

## 9. The Moon Return

- Key: the-moon-return
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/moon_return_flow.dart
- Tagline: The eye empties and fills. Receive what the cycle gives.
- Overview: At each new moon, set down one thing at dusk. At each full moon, go outside at moonrise and name what filled. The Moon Return is an ongoing Ma'at flow for the Eye of Heru cycle: emptying, restoration, and wholeness across each lunar month. Enrollment opens only at the new moon threshold.
- Confidence: Draws on new/full moon timing in Kemetic sacred calendars and the Eye of Heru cycle.

### 1. The Empty Eye

- Kind: new

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Stand under the empty sky at the new moon and set down one thing from the last cycle.

Words
"The eye has gone. The sky is cleared. I set down what the last cycle carried and let the dark receive it."

Steps
1. Step outside at dusk. You are not looking for the moon; you are standing with the empty sky.
2. Name one thing from the last lunar cycle that you are setting down.
3. Speak the line, then return inside.

Outdoor
The new moon event requires going outside at dusk, but it does not require seeing the moon.
```

### 2. The Whole Eye

- Kind: full

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Stand under the full moon sky and name one thing that filled in this cycle.

Words
"Horus has filled you complete with his eye. The Eye of Heru is whole. I receive what has been given."

Steps
1. Go outside at moonrise. If clouds block the moon, face its direction and stand under the sky anyway.
2. Stand for one minute in the full moon sky.
3. Name one thing that has filled since the new moon: In this cycle, ___ filled.
4. Speak the line, then return inside.

Outdoor
The full moon event requires going outside at moonrise. Clouds are acceptable; presence under the sky is the act.
```

## 10. The Wag

- Key: the-wag
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/the_wag_flow.dart
- Tagline: Speak their names. Set the table. They are not gone.
- Overview: The Wag is an annual Ma'at flow for the blessed dead: name ancestors, set water and bread, hold the Day 17 vigil, keep the Day 18 feast, then close with what they gave and what you will leave. It is fixed to Kemetic Month 1, following the year opening rather than a rolling thirty-day enrollment.

### 1. Begin the Naming

- Event number: 1
- Flow day: 1
- Section: The Naming
- Title: Begin the Naming
- Timing: M1 D1 · Dawn
- Words: Your name shall live at the fore of the living. May your name not perish on earth.
- Steps:
  1. Write the known names on paper: parents, grandparents, great-grandparents, and the dead you personally knew.
  2. For unknown names, write: [Name unknown] - [their relationship to you].
  3. Add one ancestor of practice, craft, community, or way of being.
  4. Read each name aloud once. Reading silently is not enough for this rite. The name spoken aloud is the name doing its work.
- Optional steps:
  1. Use the local list only as a private backup; the handwritten page is the primary act.
- Source note: The Kemite understood the ren — the name — as a constituent part of the person, as real as the body. A name not spoken eventually becomes as if it never existed. This sitting is not commemorative — it is active. Speaking the name continues what the name holds.
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 1

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The name spoken is not symbolic — in Kemetic understanding, it is the mechanism of continuation. The Wag begins with the names because without them there is no table and no feast.

Words
"Your name shall live at the fore of the living. May your name not perish on earth."

Steps
1. Write the known names on paper: parents, grandparents, great-grandparents, and the dead you personally knew.
2. For unknown names, write: [Name unknown] - [their relationship to you].
3. Add one ancestor of practice, craft, community, or way of being.
4. Read each name aloud once. Reading silently is not enough for this rite. The name spoken aloud is the name doing its work.

Optional
- Use the local list only as a private backup; the handwritten page is the primary act.
```

### 2. Extend the Names

- Event number: 2
- Flow day: 5
- Section: The Naming
- Title: Extend the Names
- Timing: M1 D5 · 11:00 local
- Words: Your name shall live at the fore of the living.
- Steps:
  1. Look at the Day 1 list and add anyone missing.
  2. Name one ancestor known only by story, or one ancestor of your practice or tradition.
  3. Place water on the surface while you add to the list. The water is already doing its work before the additional names are written.
- Source note: Kemetic offering practice acknowledged that not all names were known — the formula could address ancestors by relationship rather than personal name. The gap in the record is not a gap in the obligation. Unknown ancestors are addressed as such, not omitted.
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 5

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The naming extends to the unknown because the offering is not limited by the record. Ancestors without known names still have names — they are simply lost. Addressing them by relationship is the honest substitute.

Words
"Your name shall live at the fore of the living."

Steps
1. Look at the Day 1 list and add anyone missing.
2. Name one ancestor known only by story, or one ancestor of your practice or tradition.
3. Place water on the surface while you add to the list. The water is already doing its work before the additional names are written.
```

### 3. The Table Is Set

- Event number: 3
- Flow day: 9
- Section: The Naming
- Title: The Table Is Set
- Timing: M1 D9 · Dawn
- Words: I have not neglected the days concerning their meat offerings.
- Steps:
  1. Set water before you read any names. The water is the first provision — it goes before everything else.
  2. Read the complete list of names aloud.
  3. After each name, say: I speak your name. You live.
- Source note: Spell 125 lists neglect of the appointed offering days alongside more dramatic wrongs because the Kemite understood that the dead depended on the living's continued attention in a practical sense. The water drunk at the end is the reversion — the provision received by the one who gave it.
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 9

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Neglecting the appointed days for the dead was named in the Declaration of Innocence as a specific wrong. The table set before the feast is the household's answer to that accusation.

Words
"I have not neglected the days concerning their meat offerings."

Steps
1. Set water before you read any names. The water is the first provision — it goes before everything else.
2. Read the complete list of names aloud.
3. After each name, say: I speak your name. You live.
```

### 4. The Approach

- Event number: 4
- Flow day: 11
- Section: The Offering and the Wag
- Title: The Approach
- Timing: M1 D11 · Dawn
- Words: I have given bread to the hungry, water to the thirsty. I have made invocation-offerings for the blessed dead.
- Steps:
  1. Identify the physical surface where the Wag will be held.
  2. Identify the offerings: water first, then bread or food, and if possible something fragrant.
  3. Write one sentence about who you most want to acknowledge at the Wag.
- Optional steps:
  1. Clear the surface now and leave it empty until the vigil.
- Source note: The prt-ḫrw — the "coming forth at the voice" — was the technical term for the invocation-offering. The provision came into existence through the act of speaking it. Naming who you are speaking for makes the voice's work specific.
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 11

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The approach is the preparation — the surface chosen, the offerings gathered, the focus named. The invocation came forth through the living voice. The living voice must know who it is speaking for before it begins.

Words
"I have given bread to the hungry, water to the thirsty. I have made invocation-offerings for the blessed dead."

Steps
1. Identify the physical surface where the Wag will be held.
2. Identify the offerings: water first, then bread or food, and if possible something fragrant.
3. Write one sentence about who you most want to acknowledge at the Wag.

Optional
- Clear the surface now and leave it empty until the vigil.

Offerings
Water first. Bread or food is strongly encouraged from the approach onward; a clean surface is enough.
```

### 5. The Vigil

- Event number: 5
- Flow day: 17
- Section: The Offering and the Wag
- Title: The Vigil
- Timing: M1 D17 · Dusk
- Words: His heart joyful with the calendrical offerings of the processions of every god on the Wag-festival. His heart joyful on the Wag-festival and an eternity of years therein.
- Steps:
  1. Go to the surface prepared on Day 11.
  2. Place water, bread or food, and anything fragrant there now.
  3. Read the complete list of names aloud at dusk, without hurrying. The names read at the vigil call the dead to the table. Take the time the reading requires.
  4. Leave the offerings on the surface through the night.
- Source note: The Neferhotep Stela places joy in the heart of the dead at their calendrical provision — not as metaphor but as the expected state of the blessed dead receiving what was owed. The vigil keeps the table open for that reception.
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 17

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The vigil holds the night open for the dead. The offerings placed at dusk stay through the night because the Wag feast continues past the hour of the living's participation.

Words
"His heart joyful with the calendrical offerings of the processions of every god on the Wag-festival. His heart joyful on the Wag-festival and an eternity of years therein."

Steps
1. Go to the surface prepared on Day 11.
2. Place water, bread or food, and anything fragrant there now.
3. Read the complete list of names aloud at dusk, without hurrying. The names read at the vigil call the dead to the table. Take the time the reading requires.
4. Leave the offerings on the surface through the night.

Offerings
Water first. Bread or food is strongly encouraged from the approach onward; a clean surface is enough.
```

### 6. The Feast

- Event number: 6
- Flow day: 18
- Section: The Offering and the Wag
- Title: The Feast
- Timing: M1 D18 · Morning-midday
- Words: A thousand of bread, a thousand of beer, a thousand of cattle, a thousand of fowl, a thousand of everything sweet, a thousand of every kind of clothing - for [name], and for all those who came before.
- Steps:
  1. Return to the prepared surface and add fresh water.
  2. Read the complete list of names slowly.
  3. After each name, speak: [Name] - this bread is yours. This water is yours. You live.
  4. Sit with memory before eating. Do not rush the reversion. The bread and water that follow are the offering returning through you — the rite completing in the body.
  5. Sit with memory, then eat the bread and drink the water as reversion.
- Source note: The thousand-offering formula activated provision through speech. The reversion that followed sustained the living person who gave it. Both acts complete the same provision cycle.
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 18

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The feast is provision given and received. The thousand-offering formula activates provision through speech. The reversion — eating and drinking — closes the cycle by making the offering real in the body of the one who gave it.

Words
"A thousand of bread, a thousand of beer, a thousand of cattle, a thousand of fowl, a thousand of everything sweet, a thousand of every kind of clothing - for [name], and for all those who came before."

Steps
1. Return to the prepared surface and add fresh water.
2. Read the complete list of names slowly.
3. After each name, speak: [Name] - this bread is yours. This water is yours. You live.
4. Sit with memory before eating. Do not rush the reversion. The bread and water that follow are the offering returning through you — the rite completing in the body.
5. Sit with memory, then eat the bread and drink the water as reversion.

Offerings
Water first. Bread or food is strongly encouraged from the approach onward; a clean surface is enough.

Minimum
If you cannot keep the full feast, speak the names. Use Names spoken for the completion state.

Grace
If the vigil was missed, Wag proper is today. Keep the feast now, or speak the names as the minimum. The feast carries a 48-hour grace window.
```

### 7. What They Gave

- Event number: 7
- Flow day: 21
- Section: The Living Memory
- Title: What They Gave
- Timing: M1 D21 · Dawn
- Words: I have not stolen the cakes of the blessed dead. I carry what they gave. I offer what they carried.
- Steps:
  1. Choose one person among the dead and name one specific thing they gave you.
  2. Ask whether the living have heard that person named with this gift.
  3. Name one way to carry the gift forward more intentionally.
  4. Drink water as acknowledgment that you are still living and therefore still responsible for the transmission.
- Source note: Spell 125's prohibition against stealing the dead's cakes was practical: the dead depended on that provision. The living equivalent is the gift received from the dead — carried forward, it sustains; left dormant, it depletes.
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 21

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
To receive what the dead gave and not carry it forward is the living equivalent of stealing their cakes — the transmission ends with you instead of passing through you.

Words
"I have not stolen the cakes of the blessed dead. I carry what they gave. I offer what they carried."

Steps
1. Choose one person among the dead and name one specific thing they gave you.
2. Ask whether the living have heard that person named with this gift.
3. Name one way to carry the gift forward more intentionally.
4. Drink water as acknowledgment that you are still living and therefore still responsible for the transmission.
```

### 8. What You Will Leave

- Event number: 8
- Flow day: 25
- Section: The Living Memory
- Title: What You Will Leave
- Timing: M1 D25 · 11:00 local
- Words: A man lives when his name is spoken. Speak the names. They live.
- Steps:
  1. Write one sentence you would want spoken after your name at a future Wag.
  2. Ask whether that sentence is true of you now, partly true, or not yet true. This is not a guilt check — it is the living version of the autobiography, written while there is still time to edit it.
  3. Optional: add your own name to the continuity, not as dead, but as one who will eventually be spoken for.
- Source note: The ren principle places the living inside continuity: names received, names spoken, names eventually carried.
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 25

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
The future Wag that speaks your name will speak what was actually done, not what was intended. This sitting asks which of those two things you are currently building.

Words

Steps
1. Write one sentence you would want spoken after your name at a future Wag.
2. Ask whether that sentence is true of you now, partly true, or not yet true. This is not a guilt check — it is the living version of the autobiography, written while there is still time to edit it.
3. Optional: add your own name to the continuity, not as dead, but as one who will eventually be spoken for.
```

### 9. The Cycle Confirmed

- Event number: 9
- Flow day: 29
- Section: The Living Memory
- Title: The Cycle Confirmed
- Timing: M1 D29 · Dusk
- Words: I have made invocation-offerings for the blessed dead. Their names have been spoken. They live. I am their continuation. They are my foundation.
- Steps:
  1. Read the complete list of names one final time.
  2. Speak only the lines that are true. Each is a fact-check, not a declaration of success.
  3. Write next year's Wag date somewhere you will see it.
  4. Place water one more time. Drink it. The cycle closes.
- Source note: Harkhuf invited future passers-by to speak the offering formula in exchange for his intercession. The Wag operates on the same exchange: the living tend the dead, and the dead are available as foundation. The cycle closes with water that confirms both sides of the provision.
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 29

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The annual cycle closes with the names read one final time and the closing lines spoken only if they are true. The water drunk last is the offering returned through the body that gave it.

Words
"I have made invocation-offerings for the blessed dead. Their names have been spoken. They live. I am their continuation. They are my foundation."

Steps
1. Read the complete list of names one final time.
2. Speak only the lines that are true. Each is a fact-check, not a declaration of success.
3. Write next year's Wag date somewhere you will see it.
4. Place water one more time. Drink it. The cycle closes.

Offerings
Water first. Bread or food is strongly encouraged from the approach onward; a clean surface is enough.
```

## 11. The Decan Watch

- Key: the-decan-watch
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/the_decan_watch_flow.dart
- Tagline: The sky has been counting. Stand under it and add your count.
- Overview: At each decan boundary, go outside, look up, note one line about the sky, read the day card, and name one bearing for the next ten days. The Decan Watch is an ongoing night-sky rhythm, not a drift-repair flow.
- Confidence: Draws on ten-day decan observation and Kemetic astronomical practice.

- Required line: The Imperishable Stars never set. They mark where I am in time. The decans have counted this night for longer than I can reckon. I stand under them and add my count.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
Stand under the night sky at the opening of Sample Decan and take one bearing for the next ten days.

Outdoor
Go outside if you can. If safety, access, or weather prevents that, stand at a window or threshold and mark the completion as observed from inside.

Words
"The Imperishable Stars never set. They mark where I am in time. The decans have counted this night for longer than I can reckon. I stand under them and add my count."

Steps
1. Go outside. Phone down; open sky for at least one minute.
2. Look up. Face north first for the Imperishable Stars, then scan the full sky.
3. Speak the required line.
4. Note the sky in one line. A clouded sky is still a valid record.
5. Open the ḥꜣw day card. Read the decan name, quality, and Ma’at principle.
6. Reset intention. Name one bearing for the coming ten days.

Day Card
Open the ḥꜣw day card for this decan opening. Read the decan name, quality, and Ma’at principle.
```

## 12. The Days Outside the Year

- Key: the-days-outside-the-year
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/the_days_outside_year_flow.dart
- Tagline: Five days that do not belong to any year. Receive what is born in them.
- Overview: Close the old Kemetic year, receive the five births of Ausar, Heru Wer, Set, Aset, and Nebet-Het across the epagomenal days, then open Wep Ronpet carrying one year intention. The Days Outside the Year is annual, fixed to the calendar, and not replayed later.
- Confidence: Draws on the five epagomenal days and their deity births in the Kemetic sacred calendar.

### 0. Set Down What the Year Carried

- Event number: 0
- Kemetic date: Month 12, Day 30
- Title: Set Down What the Year Carried
- Quality: year closing
- Timing: Dusk
- Words: The year has completed its course. What it carried is named. What is finished is finished. What continues, continues.
- Steps:
  1. Name one thing the year gave you that you did not expect.
  2. Name one thing the year asked of you that you did not fully give.
  3. Name one thing that carries across the threshold into the five days.
  4. Place water while you name the three things, not after. The water witnesses the closing while it is happening.
- Source note: The year-close festival acknowledged that the civil year's work was complete before the five days began. The threshold is crossed clean — what is named is set down at the door, not carried through it.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Kemetic Date
Year 626 · Month 12 · Day 30

Timing
Dusk. This event belongs to this Kemetic calendar day only; missed days are not replayed later in the year.

Purpose
The year closes before the threshold is crossed. What is named here is what the five days receive — not a general reflection, but the three specific things: the unexpected gift, the ungiven ask, and what carries forward.

Words
"The year has completed its course. What it carried is named. What is finished is finished. What continues, continues."

What to do
1. Name one thing the year gave you that you did not expect.
2. Name one thing the year asked of you that you did not fully give.
3. Name one thing that carries across the threshold into the five days.
4. Place water while you name the three things, not after. The water witnesses the closing while it is happening.

Optional
Light a candle or oil lamp if that is safe. Water is enough.
```

### 1. Ausar - Restoration

- Event number: 1
- Kemetic date: Month 13, Day 1
- Title: Ausar - Restoration
- Birth: ausar
- Quality: restoration
- Timing: Dawn
- Words: Ausar (Osiris) shall live. The akh in Nedit shall live. What was scattered is gathered. What was diminished will endure.
- Steps:
  1. Pause at dawn before ordinary work begins. The first day of the five belongs to Ausar. Begin it before the day's ordinary logic takes over.
  2. Name one thing in the new year that needs to be gathered back.
  3. Say: This needs to be gathered back. Restoration is what I carry.
  4. Place water while you speak.
- Source note: The Osirian pattern is specifically: dispersal, gathering, restoration. Not defeat and victory — the movement of what was whole, what was scattered, and what can be gathered back. This day names what needs gathering before the new year opens.

### 2. Heru Wer - Clear Sight

- Birth: heru_wer
- Quality: clear sight
- Timing: Dawn
- Words: Heru Wer (Horus the Elder), great god, lord of the sky. The eye that sees the field from above. I receive the quality of clear sight.
- Steps:
  1. Go outside at dawn and face east. This is not symbolic — Heru Wer's quality is directional and physical. Stand outside and look at the horizon before looking at anything else.
  2. Name what you need to see from a higher position.

### 3. Set - Directed Force

- Event number: 3
- Kemetic date: Month 13, Day 3
- Title: Set - Directed Force
- Birth: set
- Quality: directed force
- Timing: Dawn
- Words: Set is born. Force and contest enter the world. What contests me sharpens me. I do not run from what is difficult. I place Set's quality in its proper position.
- Steps:
  1. Name one difficult thing you have been avoiding.
  2. Ask where force in you or around you has been misdirected. Undirected force does not disappear — it looks for somewhere to go. This day names the direction before the force finds its own.
  3. Name where that force must be pointed to defend Ma'at.
  4. Say: Directed, it defends. Unplaced, it destroys. I choose the direction.
- Source note: Set's role in the solar journey was essential: he stood at the prow of the solar barge and repelled Apophis. Force correctly placed defends Ma'at. This day gives the force its direction.

### 4. Aset - Effective Speech

- Event number: 4
- Kemetic date: Month 13, Day 4
- Title: Aset - Effective Speech
- Birth: aset
- Quality: effective speech
- Timing: Dawn
- Words: Aset (Isis) has come. She speaks to you. Knowledge alone does nothing. Speech alone does nothing. Timed and directed speech produces effect. I carry this into the new year.
- Steps:
  1. Name one true thing you know and have not yet said.
  2. Name what stops the speech: timing, fear, habit, or preparation.
  3. Name the arrangement that must be made before the word is spoken. Aset did not speak Ra's name immediately — she prepared the conditions in which the word would work. What preparation does your unspoken truth require?
  4. Say: I carry Aset's quality: to know, wait, and speak with effect.
- Source note: Aset extracted Ra's secret name through precise timing and exact speech — she spoke only what would work, at the moment it would work. The quality she embodies is not just knowledge but effective knowledge: the right thing, said rightly, at the right moment.

### 5. Nebet-Het - Threshold Witness

- Birth: nebet_het
- Quality: threshold witness

### 6. Wep Ronpet - The Year Opens

- Event number: 6
- Kemetic date: Month 1, Day 1
- Title: Wep Ronpet - The Year Opens
- Quality: year opening
- Timing: Dawn
- Words: Wep Ronpet. The year opens. I carry the five births across the first threshold. Ausar, Heru Wer, Set, Aset, Nebet-Het - restoration, sight, directed force, effective speech, threshold witness. These are what I enter this year carrying.
- Steps:
  1. Wash your hands and face at dawn before anything else. The Opening of the Year begins with purification — ordinary time restarts clean.
  2. Set fresh water on the surface and say: This water is for the year that opens.
  3. Speak each of the five names and one word for the quality you received. Not the quality in general — the specific quality as it applies to your life entering this year.
  4. Name one orienting intention for the new year. Drink the water.
- Source note: Wep Ronpet was the first day of the Kemetic civil calendar — the return of ordinary time after the threshold. The five qualities received during the five days outside were now active. The year begins carrying them.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Kemetic Date
Year 627 · Month 1 · Day 1

Timing
Dawn. This event belongs to this Kemetic calendar day only; missed days are not replayed later in the year.

Purpose
The year opens at dawn. The five qualities received across the five days are spoken one more time — as what you are carrying into the year, not as what you observed. The intention is one thing, not a list.

Words
"Wep Ronpet. The year opens. I carry the five births across the first threshold. Ausar, Heru Wer, Set, Aset, Nebet-Het - restoration, sight, directed force, effective speech, threshold witness. These are what I enter this year carrying."

What to do
1. Wash your hands and face at dawn before anything else. The Opening of the Year begins with purification — ordinary time restarts clean.
2. Set fresh water on the surface and say: This water is for the year that opens.
3. Speak each of the five names and one word for the quality you received. Not the quality in general — the specific quality as it applies to your life entering this year.
4. Name one orienting intention for the new year. Drink the water.

Share
If you share, share one word only: the quality or intention you carry into the year. Do not share the full private reflection.
```

## 13. The Open Hand

- Key: the-open-hand
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/the_open_hand_flow.dart
- Tagline: The righteous individual is he by whom others are sustained.
- Overview: Nine sittings across thirty days: see specific need, give something real beyond your circle, and confirm that provision is flowing through you like the flood. The Open Hand is outward provision, not a donation platform, public virtue feed, or replacement for The Offering Table.

### 1. Look Directly

- Event number: 1
- Flow day: 1
- Section: See the Need
- Title: Look Directly
- Timing: Day 1 · dawn + 30 min
- Words: I have given bread to the hungry, water to the thirsty, clothing to the naked, and a boat to the boatless.
- Steps:
  1. Write three names or descriptions of people or situations in your immediate environment where a specific need is present.
  2. For each, name the specific need: food, transport, shelter, attention, connection, skill, or another concrete provision.
  3. Choose one as the first act of this flow: This decan, I will give ___ to ___.
- Source note: Spell 125 and the virtue autobiographies named specific categories of provision because specificity was the evidence. "I was generous" is not a record. "I gave bread to the hungry" is. This sitting produces the record that the second decan will enact.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The Kemetic virtue autobiography recorded specific acts. Bread given to a specific hungry person. Water given to a specific thirsty one. This sitting names the specific need before deciding what to give.

Words
"I have given bread to the hungry, water to the thirsty, clothing to the naked, and a boat to the boatless."

Steps
1. Write three names or descriptions of people or situations in your immediate environment where a specific need is present.
2. For each, name the specific need: food, transport, shelter, attention, connection, skill, or another concrete provision.
3. Choose one as the first act of this flow: This decan, I will give ___ to ___.
```

### 2. The First Act

- Event number: 2
- Flow day: 5
- Section: See the Need
- Title: The First Act
- Timing: Day 5 · 11:00 local
- Words: The righteous individual is he by whom others are sustained.
- Steps:
  1. Complete the act of provision identified on Day 1 before marking this event observed. The act is the event. The logging follows the act, not the intention.
  2. Record what was given and what changed for the other person, even if the change was small.
  3. Name whether any further provision is genuinely yours to continue.
- Source note: Ptahhotep's Maxim 22 defines righteousness by whether others are actually sustained through the person. The record here is the act completed — what changed in someone else's situation because of what you did.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The act must be completed before this event is logged. Ptahhotep's standard was whether others were actually sustained through you — not whether you intended to sustain them.

Words
"The righteous individual is he by whom others are sustained."

Steps
1. Complete the act of provision identified on Day 1 before marking this event observed. The act is the event. The logging follows the act, not the intention.
2. Record what was given and what changed for the other person, even if the change was small.
3. Name whether any further provision is genuinely yours to continue.

Outward act
Complete the provision act before marking this event observed.
```

### 3. Close the First Seeing

  1. Name one local need you had been moving past without seeing.
  2. Name one act of giving in this decan that surprised you.
  3. Name one need you saw but did not act on. It carries into Decan 2 without excuse or editing.

```text
Words
```

### 7. Is Provision Flowing?

- Event number: 7
- Flow day: 21
- Section: Confirm the Flow
- Title: Is Provision Flowing?
- Timing: Day 21 · dawn + 30 min
- Words: You are Hapy who makes verdant the fields and revives the desert. The provisions of all mankind flow from you as from the flood. To do Ma’at is the breath of the nostrils.
- Steps:
  1. Map one kind of provision that entered your life this month: money, food, attention, skill, help, access, or time.
  2. Name the threshold where provision tends to stop with you — not as self-criticism but as a material observation. The threshold is where the flood is pooling rather than flowing. Naming it is the first step in moving it.
  3. Choose one way that provision can move beyond you in the final decan.
- Source note: The Hymns to Hapy praise the flood's movement as its defining virtue. Provision that reaches is provision doing its work. Provision that accumulates has lost its Hapy quality. This sitting asks where the current is pooling.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text

Words
"You are Hapy who makes verdant the fields and revives the desert. The provisions of all mankind flow from you as from the flood. To do Ma’at is the breath of the nostrils."

Steps
1. Map one kind of provision that entered your life this month: money, food, attention, skill, help, access, or time.
2. Name the threshold where provision tends to stop with you — not as self-criticism but as a material observation. The threshold is where the flood is pooling rather than flowing. Naming it is the first step in moving it.
3. Choose one way that provision can move beyond you in the final decan.
```

## 14. The Djed

- Key: the-djed
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/the_djed_flow.dart
- Tagline: What holds you upright when everything around you shakes?
- Overview: Nine sittings across thirty days: name the load-bearing elements of your life, engage what threatens them, then raise the Djed by standing upright with arms raised and declaring the spine intact.

### 1. What Is Your Spine?

- Event number: 1
- Flow day: 1
- Section: name_the_spine
- Title: What Is Your Spine?
- Timing: Day 1 · dawn + 30 min
- Words: Become established, become established, established one. Your identity will endure with people even as your identity comes to be with the gods.
- Steps:
  1. Name four or five load-bearing elements of your life right now: practices, relationships, commitments, structures, or truths.
  2. For each, mark the current condition: solid, under pressure, or wobbling.
  3. Circle the wobbling elements. These become the material for the mock battle.
- Source note: The Djed pillar was Ausar's backbone — what remained when everything else was scattered. Your spine is the same: what persists when the rest is under pressure. Name it before the mock battle begins.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The spine is what holds when everything around it is pressing. Not the aspirational structure — the actual load-bearing elements. Name what is currently holding you upright, not what should be.

Words
"Become established, become established, established one. Your identity will endure with people even as your identity comes to be with the gods."

Steps
1. Name four or five load-bearing elements of your life right now: practices, relationships, commitments, structures, or truths.
2. For each, mark the current condition: solid, under pressure, or wobbling.
3. Circle the wobbling elements. These become the material for the mock battle.
```

### 2. Name What Has Been Drifting

- Event number: 2
- Flow day: 5
- Section: name_the_spine
- Title: Name What Has Been Drifting
- Timing: Day 5 · 11:00 local
- Words: Stable in the city of the Djed. What is load-bearing must be named to hold.
- Steps:
  1. Choose the wobbling or pressured element that has been drifting longest.
  2. Name when the wobble began: what changed, stopped, or accumulated?
  3. Name one real thing that would return this element toward solid.
- Source note: Being stable in Djedut — the City of the Djed — was a specific Pyramid Texts aspiration: where the backbone held even amid what pressed against it. The longest wobble is the one most in need of that stability.

#### Canonical Detail Text

```text
Purpose
The wobble that has been going longest is usually the one that has been quietly justified, tolerated, or renamed as acceptable. This sitting finds it.

Words
"Stable in the city of the Djed. What is load-bearing must be named to hold."

Steps
1. Choose the wobbling or pressured element that has been drifting longest.
2. Name when the wobble began: what changed, stopped, or accumulated?
3. Name one real thing that would return this element toward solid.
```

  2. For every wobbling element, decide whether it should be restored, rebuilt, or released.
  3. Enter Decan 2 knowing exactly what goes into the mock battle.

## 15. The Fair Hearing

- Key: the-fair-hearing
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/maat_decan_flow.dart
- Tagline: You are a balance. If it wavers, then you will waver.
- Overview: A 30-day practice of fair judgment: hear fully before deciding, apply the same measure to those you favor and those you do not, and pronounce the decision clearly. Drawn from the nine appeals of the Eloquent Peasant.
- Confidence: Draws on Kemetic wisdom and administrative scenes of hearing, weighing, and pronounced judgment.

- Glyph: 𓄔
- Routing: Best for judgment, partiality, premature decisions, deferred decisions, and uneven hearing.
- Notes prefix: fair_hearing
- Behavior kind: maat_fair_hearing_event
- Burden: Low
- Rhythm: Decan
- Graph node slugs:
  1. maat
  2. djehuty
  3. anpu
  4. heru
### 1. Who Is Before You?

- Event number: 1
- Flow day: 1
- Section: Where Are You Called to Judge?
- Title: Who Is Before You?
- Timing: Day 1 · any time · 11:00 reminder
- Duration: 5-8 min
- Purpose: The hearing begins before you think it does. The view formed before the full account is heard is already shaping what you will hear. This sitting names that view.
- Words: Be patient, so that you may learn Ma'at. Control your own preference, so that the humble petitioner may gain.
- Steps:
  1. List the work, family, friendship, community, or self-judgment situations where your view affects an outcome.
  2. For each, mark whether you have already formed a view before fully hearing all sides.
  3. Circle the situation where your preference is strongest. This is where the fair hearing is most needed.

### 4. Hear Fully

- Event number: 4
- Flow day: 11
- Section: The Practice of Fair Hearing
- Title: Hear Fully
- Timing: Day 11 · any time · 11:00 reminder
- Duration: 5-8 min
- Purpose: Ptahhotep said the wronged person needs to unburden the heart even when not every request can be granted. The full hearing is itself a form of justice, before the decision.
- Words: Be patient when listening to the words of a petitioner. Do not dismiss him until he has completely unburdened himself.
- Steps:
  1. Before the hearing, write one sentence about what you expect to hear.
  2. Give the actual hearing. Do not interrupt. Do not signal your conclusion. The conclusion that forms while the person is still speaking is premature.
  3. Afterward, write one thing you heard that complicated or expanded your view.
- Source note: Ptahhotep's instruction is specific: do not dismiss the petitioner before they have completely unburdened themselves. A hearing that stops when the judge has enough for their existing conclusion is not a fair hearing — it is an efficient confirmation.
- Requires real-world action: true

- Timing: Day 11 · any time · 11:00 reminder

- Words: What does not belong here is set in its true place.
- Steps:

  2. Put each item where it truly belongs, discard it, or release it.
  3. Record what remains.

- Words: What is arranged must be returned to arrangement.

## 17. The Living Pattern

- Key: the-living-pattern

## 18. The House of Life

- Key: the-house-of-life
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/maat_decan_flow.dart
- Tagline: What you learn accurately, you give to those who come after.
- Overview: A 30-day scribal practice for learning accurately: write with the hand, recite with the mouth, seek those who know more, then transmit one useful piece of knowledge.
- Confidence: Draws on Per Ankh learning, scribal copying, temple practice, and careful transmission.

- Glyph: 𓉐𓋹
- Routing: Best for learning, accurate transmission, knowledge discipline, and users ready for a positive practice flow.

### 1. Enter the House of Life

- Event number: 1
- Flow day: 1
- Section: Enter the Per Ankh
- Title: Enter the House of Life
- Timing: Day 1 · morning
- Duration: 5-8 min
- Purpose: The Per Ankh was a working scriptorium — not a library where the learned went to read, but a workshop where texts were produced, tested, and transmitted. This sitting enters it as a practitioner, not a browser.
- Words: Come to me, Djehuty, that you may give advice and make me skillful in your office.
- Steps:
  1. Name what you are currently learning - a skill, craft, practice, tradition, or body of knowledge.
  2. Check the three disciplines: writing it down, speaking or reciting it, and seeking those who know more.
  3. Name the weakest of the three. This is where the practice begins.
- Source note: The Per Ankh's priests were not passive custodians — they copied, produced, interpreted, and applied what they held. Knowledge that sits in the archive without being used is not living knowledge. This sitting names what you are actively working with, and where the work is weakest.
- Requires real-world action: false

```text
3. Name the weakest of the three. This is where the practice begins.
```

- Steps:
  1. Name where you are adding: interpreting early, filling gaps, or importing your own agenda.
  2. Name where you are taking away: simplifying too far or avoiding what challenges you.
  3. Write one sentence about where accuracy is most compromised.

### 4. Write With Your Hand

- Event number: 4
- Flow day: 11
- Section: The Three Disciplines
- Title: Write With Your Hand
- Timing: Day 11 · morning
- Duration: 5-8 min
- Purpose: What you can write precisely has been learned. What you can only gesture at is still loose in the understanding. Writing is the test.
- Words: Write with your hand, recite with your mouth, and converse with those more knowledgeable than you.
- Steps:
  1. Write one complete account of something you have learned, as if the reader cannot ask you to clarify.
  2. Read it back and correct anything that depends on you standing beside the text.
  3. Name what the writing revealed about the gaps in your understanding. The gaps are not failures — they are the Per Ankh's next assignment.
- Source note: The Kemetic scribe was valued for the accuracy of the hand — the transmission that neither added nor removed. Writing what you know precisely enough for a future reader who cannot ask you to clarify is the scribal standard. Where the writing breaks down is where the learning is still incomplete.
- Requires real-world action: false

- Event number: 5
- Flow day: 15
- Section: The Three Disciplines
- Title: Recite With Your Mouth
- Timing: Day 15 · morning
- Duration: 5-5 min
- Purpose: The scribe who could recite what they copied had internalized it. The scribe who could only locate it in the text had stored it, not learned it.
- Words: Write with your hand, recite with your mouth, and converse with those more knowledgeable than you.
- Steps:
  1. Speak one concept or principle from memory, accurately and in order.
  2. Notice what is fluent and what collapses when spoken. Collapse is useful data — it shows where the understanding depends on the text rather than on itself.
  3. If someone asks a question you cannot answer accurately, write it as the next thing to learn.
- Requires real-world action: false

## 19. The Boundary Stone

- Key: the-boundary-stone
- Source: mobile/lib/features/calendar/maat_decan_flow.dart
- Tagline: What is yours ends somewhere. Know where.
- Overview: A 30-day survey of what is yours and what is not: map resources, labor, credit, and force; name where the markers moved; then restore at least one stone to its right place.
- Confidence: Draws on boundary-stone language in administrative and wisdom settings.

- Glyph: 𓊌
- Routing: Best for restraint, excess, taking more than is due, moved boundaries, credit, labor, resources, and disproportionate force.
